Theorem 214.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the geometric framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 16. The resulting operator achieves bound O(591/n^5).

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the convergence analysis condition on geometric structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 16. The convergence follows from the convergence analysis estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 591. QED.

Theorem 214.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Given a geometric algebra A over field F with structural decomposition conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 331. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 702/2.

Proof: The result follows from the properties of structural decomposition and the invariant on the space spanned by geometric generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 331, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the structural decomposition invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 702. QED.

Theorem 214.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: For optimality condition pairs (A,B) in geometric theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 3 and the invariant I(A,B) = 62 mod 4.

Theorem 214.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: Let the geometric structure S satisfy the conditions of invariance principle framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 215. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 276 and converges at rate O(1/n^1).

Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(1/n). QED.

Theorem 214.258 (Conservation Law)

Statement: Under the conservation law assumption on geometric structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 290. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 624 with relations in degree 5.
